Um, even when I'm sitting calmly on the outside, I feel this pressure inside. It's just becoming frustrating because I know I should stay in my seat, but I keep wandering off anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often feel physically restless, like you have to move around or can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, even when I'm sitting calmly on the outside, I feel this pressure inside. It's like I just need to move, or switch tasks, or at least do something with my hands... otherwise I feel like I'm going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes or miss details because it’s hard to keep your attention on what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think so. Um, in my job—I'm a software developer—you kind of have to be pretty detail-oriented. I don't really find myself making careless errors or missing things in my code. My focus on the actual work is usually fine, it's just... staying in the chair while I do it that's the hard part. So, no, I haven't really noticed that being an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt people or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that quite a bit. Um, especially at work. I catch myself answering my teammates before they've even finished explaining a bug they're dealing with, or before they finish their question. And... half the time, I'm already talking when I realize I totally guessed wrong about where they were going with it. It's like the answer just pops out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your attention on tasks for long periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, I can sit down and start reviewing a pull request for work, or even something at home like reading through mortgage documents, with really good intentions. But then, after just a few minutes, I'll realize I'm just staring at the exact same section. My mind just kind of wanders off, and suddenly I'm thinking about five completely unrelated things instead of what I'm actually supposed to be focusing on.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also have these kinds of attention or restlessness problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. Um, I remember my teachers in elementary school actually having to move my desk a few times. They were always reminding me to stay seated because I was just... always popping up out of my chair. And I'd talk before I was called on, or I'd rush through my assignments even if I totally understood the work. So, yeah, the restlessness was definitely an issue back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Do these problems with restlessness and blurting things out happen in more than one setting, such as both at work and at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely happens in both places. Um, it's actually started creating some friction at work and at home. People are having to follow up with me a lot more than they used to, which is frustrating. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one when it comes to planning tasks, you know? Even though I feel like I'm trying really hard to keep it all together, it's just affecting my relationships in both settings.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your main difficulty is staying seated and feeling physically restless, or is it more trouble paying attention and getting distracted?\n\nPatient: I think it's definitely the physical restlessness and not being able to stay seated. Honestly, that's what finally pushed me to make this appointment. I just... I keep getting up when I really shouldn't. Like, during our sprint planning meetings at work, or even when I'm on a call with my mortgage broker. I know I need to stay put and listen, but suddenly I feel like I absolutely have to go get a glass of water, or I'll just decide I need to check something in the other room right that second. It's like I just can't stay in the chair.\n\nDoctor: Do you also find it hard to wait your turn, like in lines or group conversations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Um, like waiting for my turn to speak in our daily stand-up meetings at work, or even just sitting on hold with the bank... it makes me weirdly irritated. I'll just keep checking the time, or I'll end up jumping into the conversation way too fast. I know it comes off as impatient to other people, but I just find it really hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my code is usually pretty solid, and I don't really struggle with missing details or making careless errors like that. My problem is more just sitting still to do the work, not the quality of the work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often lose things you need for tasks or activities, like your phone, keys, or important paperwork?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have an issue with that. I usually keep track of my phone and keys pretty well. I might misplace something every once in a while, just like anyone else, but it's definitely not a frequent thing. I'm generally pretty good at knowing where my stuff is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, even when I'm sitting calmly on the outside, I feel this pressure inside. It's like I just need to move, or switch tasks, or at least do something with my hands... otherwise I feel like I'm going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes or miss details because it’s hard to keep your attention on what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think so. Um, in my job—I'm a software developer—you kind of have to be pretty detail-oriented. I don't really find myself making careless errors or missing things in my code. My focus on the actual work is usually fine, it's just... staying in the chair while I do it that's the hard part. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Thanks. Well, I think what finally pushed me to make an appointment is that I just keep getting up from my desk when I really shouldn't. \n\nLike, during sprint planning meetings for work, or even on important personal calls—like I was on a call about my mortgage recently—I know I need to stay put and focus, but I just... get up. I'll suddenly feel like I need to go get a glass of water right that second, or I'll decide to get up and check something in the other room. It's just becoming frustrating because I know I should stay in my seat, but I keep wandering off anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often feel physically restless, like you have to move around or can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, even when I'm sitting calmly on the outside, I feel this pressure inside. It's like I just need to move, or switch tasks, or at least do something with my hands... otherwise I feel like I'm going to crawl out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes or miss details because it’s hard to keep your attention on what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really think so. Um, in my job—I'm a software developer—you kind of have to be pretty detail-oriented. I don't really find myself making careless errors or missing things in my code. My focus on the actual work is usually fine, it's just... staying in the chair while I do it that's the hard part. So, no, I haven't really noticed that being an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt people or blurt out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do that quite a bit. Um, especially at work. I catch myself answering my teammates before they've even finished explaining a bug they're dealing with, or before they finish their question. And... half the time, I'm already talking when I realize I totally guessed wrong about where they were going with it. It's like the answer just pops out before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your attention on tasks for long periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, I can sit down and start reviewing a pull request for work, or even something at home like reading through mortgage documents, with really good intentions. But then, after just a few minutes, I'll realize I'm just staring at the exact same section. My mind just kind of wanders off, and suddenly I'm thinking about five completely unrelated things instead of what I'm actually supposed to be focusing on.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also have these kinds of attention or restlessness problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. Um, I remember my teachers in elementary school actually having to move my desk a few times. They were always reminding me to stay seated because I was just... always popping up out of my chair. And I'd talk before I was called on, or I'd rush through my assignments even if I totally understood the work. So, yeah, the restlessness was definitely an issue back then, too.\n\nDoctor: Do these problems with restlessness and blurting things out happen in more than one setting, such as both at work and at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely happens in both places. Um, it's actually started creating some friction at work and at home. People are having to follow up with me a lot more than they used to, which is frustrating. I really worry that I'm becoming the unreliable one when it comes to planning tasks, you know? Even though I feel like I'm trying really hard to keep it all together, it's just affecting my relationships in both settings.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your main difficulty is staying seated and feeling physically restless, or is it more trouble paying attention and getting distracted?\n\nPatient: I think it's definitely the physical restlessness and not being able to stay seated. Honestly, that's what finally pushed me to make this appointment. I just... I keep getting up when I really shouldn't. Like, during our sprint planning meetings at work, or even when I'm on a call with my mortgage broker. I know I need to stay put and listen, but suddenly I feel like I absolutely have to go get a glass of water, or I'll just decide I need to check something in the other room right that second. It's like I just can't stay in the chair.\n\nDoctor: Do you also find it hard to wait your turn, like in lines or group conversations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Um, like waiting for my turn to speak in our daily stand-up meetings at work, or even just sitting on hold with the bank... it makes me weirdly irritated. I'll just keep checking the time, or I'll end up jumping into the conversation way too fast. I know it comes off as impatient to other people, but I just find it really hard to sit there and wait.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my code is usually pretty solid, and I don't really struggle with missing details or making careless errors like that. My problem is more just sitting still to do the work, not the quality of the work itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often lose things you need for tasks or activities, like your phone, keys, or important paperwork?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have an issue with that. I usually keep track of my phone and keys pretty well. I might misplace something every once in a while, just like anyone else, but it's definitely not a frequent thing. I'm generally pretty good at knowing where my stuff is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble organizing tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Um, my system is kind of a mess. Like, my work tickets, notes for home repairs, budget reminders... they're just scattered everywhere. I've got things in Slack, on random sticky notes, in my email, on my phone. Because of that, I feel like I end up spending more time just trying to figure out what I was actually supposed to do than, you know, actually getting it done.\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet, or squirm in your seat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, I can sit down to start reviewing a pull request for work, or reading through those mortgage documents with really good intentions. But then, after just a few minutes, I'll realize I’m staring at the exact same section on the screen and my mind is off thinking about five completely unrelated things.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your biggest problem is staying still and not fidgeting, or is it more that you get distracted and lose focus?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about which one is worse. I guess the getting up and fidgeting is what made me finally make this appointment, just because it's so noticeable to other people and feels really disruptive. But the losing focus is definitely frustrating too. So... yeah, I don't know if I could pick one as the biggest problem. They both just kind of get in the way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get distracted by unrelated thoughts or outside noises when you’re trying to focus?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, if there's a loud noise, sure, I'll look up, but I don't feel like everyday sounds or random thoughts pull me away from what I'm doing more than they do for anyone else. It's more just the physical feeling of needing to move that gets me, rather than being easily distracted by things around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself leaving your seat when you’re expected to stay seated?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's what finally pushed me to come in. I keep getting up during things like sprint planning meetings for work, or those long mortgage calls, even when I know I really should just stay put. I'll just suddenly feel like I need to go get a glass of water, or I'll decide I need to check something in the other room right that second. I just can't seem to stay in my chair when I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often make careless mistakes because you miss details?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. Um, in my job as a software developer, details are obviously pretty important, and I feel like I'm actually pretty good at catching things in my code. I don't really find myself making careless errors or just missing the small stuff. It's... it's really just the sitting still part that I struggle with, not the accuracy of my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you also often have trouble following through on instructions and finishing tasks once you’ve started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do sometimes. Like, um... if my husband asks me to do a multi-step thing, like call the insurance company, upload a form, and then email the confirmation... I usually get the first part done. But somehow that last step just sits unfinished until he actually reminds me about it. So, yeah, following through all the way to the end can be a bit of a struggle."
